    Headline
    acne vulgaris, alopecia, atopic dermatitis, ecchymosis, erythema, erythema multiforme, erythema nodosum, exfoliative dermatitis, flushing, hyperhidrosis, hyperkeratosis, night sweats, palmar-plantar erythrodysesthesia (hand and foot syndrome), petechiae, photosensitivity, pruritus, psoriasis, rash, skin atrophy, skin discoloration, skin hyperpigmentation, skin ulcer, urticaria, xerosis

    Headline
    cholestasis, elevated hepatic enzymes, hepatitis, hepatomegaly, hyperbilirubinemia, hypoalbuminemia, jaundice
    General Information
    Hepatotoxicity (e.g., elevated hepatic enzymes) has been reported with nilotinib therapy. Closely monitor patients with hepatic impairment for QT prolongation as nilotinib systemic exposure may be increased in these patients. Obtain liver function tests monthly, following dosage adjustments, and as clinically indicated. Therapy interruption and a dosage reduction are recommended in patients who develop grade 3 or higher bilirubin or transaminase level elevations during nilotinib therapy. Increases in total bilirubin (59%; grade 3 or 4, 4%), ALT (72%; grade 3 or 4, 4%), and AST (47%; grade 3 or 4, 1%) levels occurred in patients with newly diagnosed chronic phase (CP) Philadelphia chromosome-positive chronic myelogenous leukemia (Ph+CML) who received nilotinib in a randomized, comparative trial. Hepatotoxicity reported in patients with CP or accelerated phase Ph+CML resistant or intolerant to at least 1 prior therapy including imatinib who received nilotinib in a single-arm trial included grade 3 or 4 decreased albumin concentrations (hypoalbuminemia) (3% to 4%) and grade 3 or 4 elevations in total bilirubin (7% to 9%), ALT (4%), AST (2% to 3%), and alkaline phosphatase (1% or less) levels. Additionally, hyperbilirubinemia and increased ALT and AST levels were reported in 10% or higher of nilotinib-treated patients; increased alkaline phosphatase and gamma-glutamyltransferase (GGT) levels and abnormal hepatic function were reported in 1% to 10% of nilotinib-treated patients; and hepatotoxicity, toxic hepatitis, and jaundice were reported in 0.1% to 1% of nilotinib-treated patients in clinical studies. Single-events of cholestasis, hepatomegaly, and increased unconjugated bilirubin level have also occurred with nilotinib therapy. Grade 3 or 4 elevated bilirubin, AST, and ALT levels were reported at a higher frequency in pediatric patients compared with adults. In pooled results from 2 studies, hyperbilirubinemia and elevated hepatic enzymes (i.e., increased AST and ALT levels) were reported in greater than 20% of pediatric patients aged 2 to 18 years with CP-CML; grade 3 of 4 hyperbilirubinemia (16%) and increased AST (2.9%) and ALT (10%) levels occurred in these patients.

    Headline
    candidiasis, folliculitis, infection, influenza, pharyngitis
    General Information
    Infection including (naso) pharyngitis (15% to 27%) and upper respiratory tract infection (10% to 17%) has been reported with nilotinib therapy in clinical trials. Pharyngitis (27%), influenza (13%), and upper respiratory tract infection (17%; grade 3 or 4, less than 1%) occurred in patients with newly diagnosed chronic phase (CP) Philadelphia chromosome-positive chronic myelogenous leukemia (Ph+ CML) who received nilotinib in a randomized, comparative trial. Infectious events reported in patients with CP or accelerated phase Ph+ CML resistant or intolerant to at least 1 prior therapy including imatinib who received nilotinib in a single-arm trial included nasopharyngitis (15% to 24%; grade 3 or 4, less than 1%) and upper respiratory tract infection (10% to 12%). Additionally, folliculitis was reported in 1% to 10% of nilotinib-treated patients and bronchitis, candidiasis (e.g., oral candidiasis), pneumonia, urinary tract infection, and influenza-like illness were reported in 0.1% to 1% of nilotinib-treated patients in clinical studies. Single-events of hepatitis B virus reactivation, sepsis, subcutaneous abscess, anal abscess, furuncle, and tinea pedis have also occurred with nilotinib therapy. In pooled results from 2 studies, upper respiratory tract infection was reported in greater than 20% of pediatric patients aged 2 to 18 years with CP-CML.

    General Information
    Nervous system adverse events have been reported with nilotinib therapy in clinical trials.     Headline
    hyperamylasemia, pancreatitis
    General Information
    Pancreatitis and elevated lipase and amylase (hyperamylasemia) levels have been reported with nilotinib use. Evaluate lipase and amylase levels monthly or as clinically indicated. In patients who have abdominal pain and elevated lipase levels, hold nilotinib and evaluate for pancreatitis. Therapy interruption and a dosage reduction are recommended in patients who develop grade 3 or higher lipase or amylase level elevations during nilotinib therapy. An elevated lipase level was reported in 28% (grade 3 or 4, 9%) of patients with newly diagnosed chronic phase (CP) Philadelphia chromosome-positive chronic myelogenous leukemia (Ph+ CML) who received nilotinib in a randomized, comparative trial. Grade 3 or 4 elevated lipase levels were reported in 18% of patients with CP-CML and accelerated phase CML who were resistant or intolerant to at least 1 prior therapy including imatinib and who received nilotinib in a single-arm trial. Additionally, pancreatitis and elevated amylase levels each were reported in 1% to 10% of nilotinib-treated patients in clinical studies.

Description
  Text
  Nilotinib is a BCR-ABL tyrosine kinase inhibitor. It is indicated for the treatment of pediatric patients aged 1 year and older (Tasigna capsule formulation and generic equivalents only) and adults with newly diagnosed chronic phase (CP)-Philadelphia chromosome positive chronic myeloid leukemia (Ph+ CML), adult patients with CP-Ph+ CML or accelerated phase (AP)-Ph+ CML resistant to or intolerant to prior therapy that included imatinib, and pediatric patients aged 1 year and older (Tasigna capsule formulation and generic equivalents only) with CP-Ph+ CML or AP-Ph+ CML resistant or intolerant to prior tyrosine kinase inhibitor therapy. Eligible adult patients with CP-Ph+ CML who have received nilotinib for a minimum of 3 years and have achieved a sustained molecular response (MR4.5) may be considered for treatment discontinuation. Treatment with nilotinib may cause QT prolongation; sudden death has been reported.

Mechanism Of Action
  Text
  Nilotinib is an inhibitor of the BCR-ABL kinase. Nilotinib binds to and stabilizes the inactive conformation of the kinase domain of ABL protein. In vitro, nilotinib inhibited BCR-ABL mediated proliferation of murine leukemic cell lines and human cell lines derived from patients with Ph+ CML. Under the conditions of the assays, nilotinib was able to overcome imatinib resistance resulting from BCR-ABL kinase mutations. Nilotinib inhibited the autophosphorylation of the following kinases: BCR-ABL, PDGFR, c-KIT, CSF-1R, and DDR1.

Pharmacokinetics
  Route Specific Pharmacokinetics
    Name
    Oral Route
    Description
    The relative bioavailability of nilotinib capsules (Tasigna and generic equivalents) is approximately 50% compared with an oral drink solution. However, a single dose of two 200-mg nilotinib capsules (each dispersed in 1 teaspoon of applesauce and administered within 15 minutes) was bioequivalent to a single dose of two 200-mg intact capsules. The steady-state nilotinib exposure of the capsule formulation is dose-dependent and exhibits less than dose-proportional increases in systemic exposure at dosages higher than 400 mg once or twice daily. The increase in nilotinib exposure between the first dose and steady state is about 2-fold for daily dosing and 3.8-fold for twice daily dosing. The Tmax is reached 3 hours after nilotinib capsule (Tasigna and generic equivalents) administration and 3.3 hours after nilotinib d-tartrate capsule (Nilceya) administration. Following the capsule administration of nilotinib 400 mg PO twice daily, the steady-state mean Cmax and AUC values were 2,260 nanog/mL (CV, 35%) and 18,000 nanog x hour/mL (CV, 33%), respectively. Following the capsule administration of nilotinib 300 mg PO twice daily, the steady-state mean Cmax and AUC values were 1,540 nanog/mL (CV, 48%) and 13,337 nanog x hour/mL (CV, 46%), respectively. Steady-state concentrations are achieved by day 8. The Tmax is reached at 2.7 hours following a single dose tablet administration of nilotinib 190 mg in fasted subjects. Following the tablet administration of nilotinib 142 mg PO twice daily, the steady-state mean Cmax and AUC values were 2,071 +/- 761 ng/mL and 14,525 +/- 5,690 ng x hour/mL, respectively, in fasted patients. Following the tablet administration of nilotinib 190 mg PO twice daily, the steady-state mean Cmax and AUC values were 2,229 +/- 790 nanog/mL and 15,662 +/- 5,738 nanog x hour/mL, respectively, in fasted patients. The median time to reach Tmax is 2 hours after nilotinib orally disintegrating tablet administration in a fasted state. Following a single-dose 120 mg administration, the Cmax and AUC values were 848 +/- 315 nanog/mL and 13,240 +/- 6,030 nanog x hour/mL, respectively, in fasted patients. Following a single-dose 160 mg administration, the Cmax and AUC values were 1,042 +/- 383 nanog/mL and 15,460 +/- 6,020 nanog x hour/mL, respectively, in fasted patients.
 
Effect of Food (Tasigna capsule formulation and generic equivalents): The bioavailability of nilotinib is significantly increased by food. The AUC value was increased 82% when nilotinib was taken 30 minutes prior to a high-fat meal (800 to 1,000 calories with 50% of total caloric content as fat) compared with the fasted state.
Effect of Food (Nilceya capsule formulation): Nilotinib AUC increased by 68% with a high-fat meal (approximately 800 to 1,000 calories, 50% fat) and increased by 53% with a low-fat meal (approximately 400 to 500 calories, 25% fat) compared to the fasted state following a single 200 mg dose in healthy subjects.
Effect of Food (Danziten tablet formulation): No clinically significant differences in nilotinib exposure were observed following administration of 142 mg or 190 mg with a high-fat meal or a low-fat meal compared to fasted subjects.
Effect of Food (Cavhanza orally disintegrating tablet formulation): Nilotinib AUC increased by no more than 14% and 13%, respectively, when administered with a high-fat (50% fat), moderate-fat (37% fat), or low-fat (25% fat) meal relative to the fasted state.
  Special Populations
    Name
    Hepatic Impairment
    Description
    Following the capsule administration of a single dose of nilotinib 200 mg (tablet dose equivalent of 95 mg and orally disintegrating tablet equivalent of 80 mg), the mean AUC values increased 1.4-fold, 1.4-fold, and 1.6-fold in subjects with mild (Child-Pugh class A), moderate (Child-Pugh class B) and severe (Child-Pugh class C) hepatic impairment, respectively, compared with subjects who had normal hepatic function.
    Name
    Pediatrics
    Description
    Following the capsule administration of nilotinib 230 mg/m2 PO twice daily, steady-state AUC values were within 2-fold the AUC values in adults who received nilotinib 400 mg PO twice daily. The steady-state trough concentrations were not significantly different between pediatric age groups (age range, 2 years to less than 18 years) or for pediatric patients with newly diagnosed or resistant/intolerant chronic myelogenous leukemia. Body surface area correlated with nilotinib clearance; this was the primary factor responsible for the pharmacokinetic differences between pediatric and adult patients.
    Name
    Geriatric
    Description
    Age does not significantly affect the pharmacokinetic parameters of nilotinib.
    Name
    Gender Differences
    Description
    Gender does not significantly affect the pharmacokinetic parameters of nilotinib.
    Name
    Ethnic Differences
    Description
    Race/ethnicity does not significantly affect the pharmacokinetic parameters of nilotinib.
    Name
    Obesity
    Description
    Body weight does not significantly affect the pharmacokinetic parameters of nilotinib.
    Name
    Other
    Description
    Total Gastrectomy
Median steady-state nilotinib capsule and nilotinib orally disintegrating tablet trough concentrations were decreased by 53% in patients who had received a total gastrectomy procedure compared with patients who had not undergone this surgery.
  Text
  Nilotinib is administered orally. It is approximately 98% protein bound in serum; the blood to serum ratio is 0.68. Nilotinib is metabolized via oxidation by CYP3A4 (major) and CYP2C8 (minor) isoenzymes in the liver; no active metabolites have been identified. The elimination half-life of nilotinib capsules is approximately 17 hours (CV, 69%) and the mean apparent clearance is about 29 L/hour (CV, 61%). The elimination half-life of nilotinib tablets is approximately 14 hours. Following a single 160 mg dose of nilotinib orally disintegrating tablet, the elimination half-life is 13.6 hours (CV, 36.4%) and the mean clearance is 10.9 L/hour (CV, 44.8%). Following a single radiolabeled nilotinib dose, 93% of the dose was eliminated mainly in the feces within 7 days, and parent drug accounted for 69% of the dose.
 
Affected cytochrome P450 isoenzymes and transporters: CYP3A4, CYP2C8, CYP2D6, P-gp, UGT1A1
Nilotinib is a moderately sensitive substrate of CYP3A4. Concomitant administration with strong CYP3A4 inhibitors or inducers may increase or decrease nilotinib concentrations significantly. In vitro, nilotinib is an inhibitor of CYP2C8, CYP2D6, P-glycoprotein (P-gp) and UGT1A1 and induces CYP2B6 and CYP2C8. Additionally, nilotinib is a substrate and P-gp in vitro. Nilotinib capsules (Tasigna and generic equivalents) are also a moderate CYP3A4 inhibitor based on midazolam (a substrate of CYP3A4) interaction.

Administration
  General Information
  NOTE: Nilotinib is available in different formulations, dosage forms, and strengths that are approved with different indications and recommended dosages; do not substitute nilotinib tablets with other nilotinib products on a milligram per milligram basis.
For storage information, see the specific product information within the How Supplied section.
Hazardous Drugs Classification
NIOSH 2024 List: Table 2
Observe and exercise appropriate precautions for handling, preparation, administration, and disposal of hazardous drugs.
Use gloves to handle; double chemotherapy gloves and a protective gown are needed if manipulation is necessary. Cutting, crushing, or otherwise manipulating tablets/capsules will increase exposure.
Emetic Risk
Minimal/Low
Administer prn antiemetics as necessary.
  Route Specific Administrations
    Administration Details
      Name
      Extemporaneous Compounding-Oral
      Description
      TASIGNA CAPSULES AND GENERIC EQUIVALENTS
For patients unable to swallow nilotinib capsules, capsule contents may be dispersed in 1 teaspoonful (5 mL) of applesauce.
Take the mixture immediately or within 15 minutes; do not store for future use.
DANZITEN TABLETS
For patients unable to swallow whole tablets, nilotinib may be dispersed in liquid for administration immediately prior to use.
Place the prescribed dose into a small cup containing a small amount (1 to 2 teaspoons, or just enough to cover the medication) of room temperature liquid (e.g., water, milk, apple juice, pineapple juice, cranberry juice).
After approximately 1 minute and when the medication is fully dispersed, swirl the solution and consume the entire quantity immediately (within 15 minutes); do not store for future use.
    Name
    Oral Administration
    Description
    CAPSULES:
In pediatric patients receiving nilotinib capsules (Tasigna and generic equivalents), dose should be rounded to the nearest 50 mg dose (maximum single dose, 400 mg); different strength capsules may need to be combined to make the dose.
Take on an empty stomach; no food should be consumed for at least 1 hour after the dose is taken or for at least 2 hours before the dose is taken.
Swallow whole with water.
For twice daily dosing, separate doses by approximately 12 hours.
Do not drink grapefruit juice during treatment.
Take H2-blockers 10 hours before taking nilotinib or 2 hours after; separate the nilotinib dose from antacids by at least 2 hours.
If a dose is missed, take the next scheduled dose at the regular time; do not take 2 doses at the same time.
TABLETS:
Take with or without food.
Swallow whole with water.
For twice daily dosing, separate doses by approximately 12 hours.
Do not drink grapefruit juice during treatment.
Take H2-blockers 10 hours before taking nilotinib or 2 hours after; separate the nilotinib dose from antacids by at least 2 hours.
If a dose is missed, take the next scheduled dose at the regular time; do not take 2 doses at the same time.
ORALLY DISINTEGRATING TABLETS:
Use dry hands when opening the bottle and handing tablets.
Place tablet(s) on top of the tongue and allow to disintegrate, then swallow with silva; administration with liquid is not necessary.
Tablets may be chewed prior to swallowing with saliva or swallowed intact with water; do not crush tablets prior to administration.
For twice daily dosing, separate doses by approximately 12 hours.
Do not drink grapefruit juice during treatment.
If a dose is missed, take the next scheduled dose at the regular time; do not take 2 doses at the same time.

Dosage Adjustment Guidelines
  Hepatic Impairment
  Baseline Hepatic Impairment (adults only)
Newly diagnosed Philadelphia chromosome-positive (Ph+) chronic myelogenous leukemia (CML)
TASIGNA CAPSULES AND GENERIC EQUIVALENTS
Any hepatic impairment: Initiate nilotinib at 200 mg PO twice daily. Escalate dosage to the recommended dosage of 300 mg twice daily based on individual patient tolerability.
NILCEYA CAPSULES
Any hepatic impairment: Initiate nilotinib at 200 mg PO twice daily. Escalate dosage to the recommended dosage of 300 mg twice daily based on individual patient tolerability.
DANZITEN TABLETS
Any hepatic impairment: Initiate nilotinib at 95 mg PO twice daily. Escalate dosage to the recommended dosage of 142 mg twice daily based on individual patient tolerability.
CAVHANZA ORALLY DISINTEGRATING TABLETS
Any hepatic impairment: Initiate nilotinib at 80 mg PO twice daily. Escalate dosage to the recommended dosage of 120 mg twice daily based on individual patient tolerability.
Resistant or intolerant Ph+ CML
TASIGNA CAPSULES AND GENERIC EQUIVALENTS
Mild or moderate hepatic impairment (Child-Pugh class A or B): Initiate nilotinib at 300 mg PO twice daily. Escalate dosage to the recommended dosage of 400 mg twice daily based on individual patient tolerability.
Severe hepatic impairment (Child-Pugh class C): Initiate nilotinib at 200 mg PO twice daily. Escalate dosage to 300 mg twice daily and then to the recommended dosage of 400 mg twice daily based on individual patient tolerability.
NILCEYA CAPSULES
Mild or moderate hepatic impairment (Child-Pugh class A or B): Initiate nilotinib at 300 mg PO twice daily. Escalate dosage to the recommended dosage of 400 mg twice daily based on individual patient tolerability.
Severe hepatic impairment (Child-Pugh class C): Initiate nilotinib at 200 mg PO twice daily. Escalate dosage to 300 mg twice daily and then to the recommended dosage of 400 mg twice daily based on individual patient tolerability.
DANZITEN TABLETS
Mild or moderate hepatic impairment (Child-Pugh class A or B): Initiate nilotinib at 142 mg PO twice daily. Escalate dosage to the recommended dosage of 190 mg twice daily based on individual patient tolerability.
Severe hepatic impairment (Child-Pugh class C): Initiate nilotinib at 95 mg PO twice daily. Escalate dosage to 142 mg twice daily and then to the recommended dosage of 190 mg twice daily based on individual patient tolerability.
CAVHANZA ORALLY DISINTEGRATING TABLETS
Mild or moderate hepatic impairment (Child-Pugh class A or B): Initiate nilotinib at 120 mg PO twice daily. Escalate dosage to the recommended dosage of 160 mg twice daily based on individual patient tolerability.
Severe hepatic impairment (Child-Pugh class C): Initiate nilotinib at 80 mg PO twice daily. Escalate dosage to the recommended dosage of 120 mg twice daily and then 160 mg twice daily based on individual patient tolerability.
 
Treatment-Related Hepatotoxicity
Adults
TASIGNA CAPSULES AND GENERIC EQUIVALENTS
Grade 3 or higher elevated bilirubin or transaminase levels: Hold nilotinib therapy. Once toxicity is resolved to grade 1 or less, resume nilotinib at 400 mg PO once daily.
NILCEYA CAPSULES
Grade 3 or higher elevated bilirubin or transaminase levels: Hold nilotinib therapy. Once toxicity is resolved to grade 1 or less, resume nilotinib at 400 mg PO once daily.
DANZITEN TABLETS
Grade 3 or higher elevated bilirubin or transaminase levels: Hold nilotinib therapy. Once toxicity is resolved to grade 1 or less, resume nilotinib at 190 mg PO once daily.
CAVHANZA ORALLY DISINTEGRATING TABLETS
Grade 3 or higher elevated bilirubin or transaminase levels: Hold nilotinib therapy. Once toxicity is resolved to grade 1 or less, resume nilotinib at 160 mg PO once daily.
Pediatric patients
TASIGNA CAPSULES AND GENERIC EQUIVALENTS
Grade 2 or higher elevated bilirubin level OR grade 3 or higher transaminase levels: Hold nilotinib therapy. Once toxicity is resolved to grade 1 or less, resume nilotinib at 230 mg/m2 PO once daily if previous dosage was nilotinib 230 mg/m2 PO twice daily. Discontinue therapy in pediatric patients who were receiving a previous dose of nilotinib 230 mg/m2 PO once daily and recovery to grade 1 or less takes longer than 28 days.
  Renal Impairment
  Specific guidelines for dosage adjustments in renal impairment are not available; it appears that no dosage adjustments are needed.
